Registers new user accounts in a relational database and authenticates them\n * again later, on top of a JDBC {@link Connection} the caller already owns.\n *\n * <h2>Password handling</h2>\n *\n * A password is never stored, never returned, and never concatenated into SQL.\n * What goes into the database is a self-describing password digest:\n *\n * <pre>pbkdf2-sha256$600000$Base64(salt)$Base64(digest)</pre>\n *\n * <ul>\n *   <li><b>Key stretching.</b> PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 with 600,000 iterations\n *       (OWASP 2023 guidance for this primitive), so an offline crack of a\n *       stolen table costs ~0.9s per guess on one core of this machine.</li>\n *   <li><b>Per-account salt.</b> 16 fresh bytes from {@link SecureRandom} per\n *       registration, so two users with the same password get different rows\n *       and one precomputed rainbow table cannot cover the table.</li>\n *   <li><b>Stored parameters.</b> Algorithm and iteration count travel with the\n *       digest, so {@link #authenticate} verifies against the cost that was in\n *       force when the account was created, and the default can be raised later\n *       without invalidating existing accounts.</li>\n *   <li><b>Constant-time comparison.</b> Candidate and stored digests are\n *       compared with {@link MessageDigest}#isEqual, which does not return early\n *       on the first differing byte.</li>\n *   <li><b>No user-enumeration timing leak.</b> An unknown username triggers the\n *       same PBKDF2 run against a throwaway digest, so \"no such user\" and \"wrong\n *       password\" take the same time to answer.</li>\n *   <li><b>char[] in, zeroed scratch out.</b> Passwords are taken as\n *       {@code char[]} so callers can wipe them (see {@link #clear}) instead of\n *       parking the secret in an immutable {@code String}; the derived key bytes\n *       the library allocates are zeroed as soon as they are compared. Java\n *       cannot guarantee that a {@code String} password was never interned,\n *       cached, or paged to swap, which is exactly why {@code String} is the\n *       wrong type for this parameter.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <h2>What the caller still owns</h2>\n *\n * The {@code Connection} is never closed here. Transport (TLS), account\n * lockout, rate limiting, and the user-facing wording of a failed login are\n * outside this class' scope and are required for a real deployment; see README.\n */\npublic final class UserRegistration {\n\n    /** Target table. A compile-time constant, never derived from user input. */\n    public static final String TABLE = \"app_users\";\n\n    private UserRegistration() {\n    }\n\n    /** Result of {@link UserRegistry#register}. */\n    public enum RegistrationOutcome {\n        /** The account row was written. */\n        CREATED,\n        /** Another account already owns this username; nothing was written. */\n        USERNAME_ALREADY_EXISTS\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Result of {@link UserRegistry#authenticate}. {@code NO_SUCH_USER} and\n     * {@code BAD_PASSWORD} must be rendered with one identical message by the\n     * caller, so the distinction stays useful for server-side logging and\n     * migration tooling without becoming an enumeration oracle.\n     */\n    public enum AuthenticationOutcome {\n        AUTHENTICATED,\n        NO_SUCH_USER,\n        BAD_PASSWORD\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Salted, stretched password digests. Instances are thread-safe: the\n     * iteration count is fixed at construction and {@link SecureRandom} is\n     * safe for concurrent use.\n     */\n    public static final class PasswordHasher {\n\n        /** PBKDF2 pseudo-random function, as named by the JCA. */\n        public static final String JCA_ALGORITHM = \"PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256\";\n        /** Tag written into the stored digest. */\n        public static final String FORMAT_ID = \"pbkdf2-sha256\";\n\n        /**\n         * Iterations stamped on new accounts. 600,000 is OWASP's 2023 floor for\n         * PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256; it is not a maximum, and raising it is a\n         * non-breaking change because each row keeps its own count.\n         */\n        public static final int DEFAULT_ITERATIONS = 600_000;\n\n        /** Floor accepted for a stored digest, so a hostile row cannot ask for 1 round. */\n        public static final int MIN_ITERATIONS = 1_000;\n        /** Ceiling accepted for a stored digest, so a hostile row cannot ask for 10^9 rounds. */\n        public static final int MAX_ITERATIONS = 10_000_000;\n\n        public static final int SALT_BYTES = 16;\n        public static final int DIGEST_BYTES = 32;\n\n        /**\n         * Longest password accepted. PBKDF2 cost is linear in password length,\n         * so an unbounded field would let anyone make registration arbitrarily\n         * expensive. 1024 characters is far above any human-chosen secret.\n         */\n        public static final int MAX_PASSWORD_CHARS = 1_024;\n\n        private static final String SEPARATOR = \"$\";\n\n        private final int iterations;\n        private final SecureRandom random = new SecureRandom();\n\n        public PasswordHasher() {\n            this(DEFAULT_ITERATIONS);\n        }\n\n        public PasswordHasher(int iterations) {\n            if (iterations < MIN_ITERATIONS || iterations > MAX_ITERATIONS) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\n                        \"iterations must be in [\" + MIN_ITERATIONS + \", \" + MAX_ITERATIONS + \"]\");\n            }\n            this.iterations = iterations;\n        }\n\n        public int getIterations() {\n            return iterations;\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * Produces the value to store for {@code password}. The caller's array\n         * is not modified: wipe it with {@link #clear} as soon as you are done\n         * with it.\n         *\n         * @throws IllegalArgumentException if the password is empty or longer\n         *                                  than {@link #MAX_PASSWORD_CHARS}\n         */\n        public String hash(char[] password) {\n            requireUsablePassword(password);\n            byte[] salt = new byte[SALT_BYTES];\n            random.nextBytes(salt);\n            byte[] digest = derive(password, salt, iterations);\n            try {\n                Base64.Encoder encoder = Base64.getEncoder();\n                return FORMAT_ID + SEPARATOR + iterations + SEPARATOR\n                        + encoder.encodeToString(salt) + SEPARATOR + encoder.encodeToString(digest);\n            } finally {\n                Arrays.fill(digest, (byte) 0);\n            }\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * Checks {@code password} against a stored digest. Returns\n         * {@code false} for a malformed or unsupported stored value rather than\n         * throwing, so one corrupt row cannot turn into a stack trace that\n         * reaches a login page.\n         */\n        public boolean matches(char[] password, String storedDigest) {\n            if (password == null || password.length == 0 || storedDigest == null) {\n                return false;\n            }\n            if (password.length > MAX_PASSWORD_CHARS) {\n                return false;\n            }\n            Stored parsed;\n            try {\n                parsed = Stored.parse(storedDigest);\n            } catch (IllegalArgumentException malformed) {\n                return false;\n            }\n            byte[] candidate = derive(password, parsed.salt, parsed.iterations);\n            try {\n                // isEqual is constant-time for equal-length inputs and\n                // short-circuits only on the length difference, which the\n                // stored format already fixes at DIGEST_BYTES.\n                return MessageDigest.isEqual(parsed.digest, candidate);\n            } finally {\n                Arrays.fill(candidate, (byte) 0);\n            }\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * Overwrites {@code password} before the caller drops it. Best effort by\n         * design: it narrows the window in which the secret sits in reachable\n         * heap, it cannot retract a copy the JVM or your own code already made.\n         */\n        public static void clear(char[] password) {\n            if (password != null) {\n                Arrays.fill(password, '\\0');\n            }\n        }\n\n        private static void requireUsablePassword(char[] password) {\n            if (password == null || password.length == 0) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"password must not be empty\");\n            }\n            if (password.length > MAX_PASSWORD_CHARS) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\n                        \"password must not exceed \" + MAX_PASSWORD_CHARS + \" characters\");\n            }\n        }\n\n        /** Runs PBKDF2. The {@link PBEKeySpec} is cleared on every path. */\n        private static byte[] derive(char[] password, byte[] salt, int iterations) {\n            PBEKeySpec spec = new PBEKeySpec(password, salt, iterations, DIGEST_BYTES * 8);\n            try {\n                SecretKeyFactory factory = SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(JCA_ALGORITHM);\n                return factory.generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();\n            } catch (java.security.NoSuchAlgorithmException | java.security.InvalidKeySpecException e) {\n                // Absent PBKDF2 or a rejected key spec is a deployment fault,\n                // not a condition a caller can recover from per-request.\n                throw new IllegalStateException(JCA_ALGORITHM + \" is unavailable on this JVM\", e);\n            } finally {\n                spec.clearPassword();\n            }\n        }\n\n        /** One parsed {@code pbkdf2-sha256$iterations$salt$digest} value. */\n        private static final class Stored {\n\n            private final int iterations;\n            private final byte[] salt;\n            private final byte[] digest;\n\n            private Stored(int iterations, byte[] salt, byte[] digest) {\n                this.iterations = iterations;\n                this.salt = salt;\n                this.digest = digest;\n            }\n\n            static Stored parse(String storedDigest) {\n                String[] parts = storedDigest.split(\"\\\\\" + SEPARATOR, -1);\n                if (parts.length != 4 || !FORMAT_ID.equals(parts[0])) {\n                    throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"unrecognised password digest format\");\n                }\n                int iterationCount;\n                try {\n                    iterationCount = Integer.parseInt(parts[1]);\n                } catch (NumberFormatException notANumber) {\n                    throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"password digest has no iteration count\");\n                }\n                if (iterationCount < MIN_ITERATIONS || iterationCount > MAX_ITERATIONS) {\n                    throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"iteration count out of range: \" + iterationCount);\n                }\n                // Base64.getDecoder() is the strict variant: it rejects foreign\n                // characters and missing padding, unlike the MIME decoder.\n                byte[] salt = decode(parts[2]);\n                byte[] digest = decode(parts[3]);\n                if (salt.length != SALT_BYTES) {\n                    throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"salt must be \" + SALT_BYTES + \" bytes\");\n                }\n                if (digest.length != DIGEST_BYTES) {\n                    throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"digest must be \" + DIGEST_BYTES + \" bytes\");\n                }\n                return new Stored(iterationCount, salt, digest);\n            }\n\n            private static byte[] decode(String base64) {\n                try {\n                    return Base64.getDecoder().decode(base64);\n                } catch (IllegalArgumentException notBase64) {\n                    throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"password digest is not valid Base64\", notBase64);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Reads and writes account rows on one connection.\n     *\n     * <p>Every statement that touches user-supplied data is a\n     * {@link PreparedStatement} with {@code ?} placeholders, so a username such\n     * as {@code ' OR 1=1 --} is stored and matched as a literal username\n     * instead of becoming part of the statement text. Only {@link #TABLE}, a\n     * compile-time constant, is ever concatenated into SQL.</p>\n     *\n     * <p>Instances are <em>not</em> thread-safe, because the underlying\n     * {@link Connection} is not. Use one registry per connection (typically one\n     * per request).</p>\n     */\n    public static final class UserRegistry {\n\n        /** Usernames are ASCII labels: length-bounded and free of quoting and spacing surprises. */\n        public static final Pattern USERNAME_PATTERN = Pattern.compile(\"[A-Za-z0-9._-]{3,64}\");\n\n        private static final String INSERT_SQL =\n                \"INSERT INTO \" + TABLE + \" (username, password_hash, created_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?)\";\n        private static final String SELECT_DIGEST_SQL =\n                \"SELECT password_hash FROM \" + TABLE + \" WHERE username = ?\";\n        private static final String EXISTS_SQL =\n                \"SELECT 1 FROM \" + TABLE + \" WHERE username = ?\";\n        private static final String CREATE_TABLE_SQL =\n                \"CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS \" + TABLE + \" (\"\n                        + \"id BIGINT GENERATED BY DEFAULT AS IDENTITY PRIMARY KEY, \"\n                        + \"username VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL, \"\n                        + \"password_hash VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL, \"\n                        + \"created_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL)\";\n        private static final String CREATE_INDEX_SQL =\n                \"CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ux_\" + TABLE + \"_username ON \" + TABLE + \" (username)\";\n\n        /** Password tried against the guard digest when a username is unknown. */\n        private static final char[] GUARD_PASSWORD = \"timing-guard-password\".toCharArray();\n\n        private final Connection connection;\n        private final PasswordHasher hasher;\n        private final boolean commitOwnWork;\n\n        /** A digest no real account owns, for equalising the unknown-user path. */\n        private final String guardDigest;\n\n        public UserRegistry(Connection connection, PasswordHasher hasher) {\n            this(connection, hasher, true);\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * @param commitOwnWork when {@code true} and the connection has manual\n         *                      commits, a successful insert is committed here.\n         *                      Set it to {@code false} if the surrounding unit\n         *                      of work decides when to commit; nothing else\n         *                      about this method's behaviour changes.\n         */\n        public UserRegistry(Connection connection, PasswordHasher hasher, boolean commitOwnWork) {\n            if (connection == null || hasher == null) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"connection and hasher are required\");\n            }\n            this.connection = connection;\n            this.hasher = hasher;\n            this.commitOwnWork = commitOwnWork;\n            this.guardDigest = hasher.hash(GUARD_PASSWORD);\n        }\n\n        public Connection getConnection() {\n            return connection;\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * Creates {@value #TABLE} and its unique username index if absent.\n         * Idempotent; call it once at startup, or run the DDL in\n         * {@code README.md} through a migration instead.\n         */\n        public void ensureSchema() throws SQLException {\n            try (Statement statement = connection.createStatement()) {\n                statement.execute(CREATE_TABLE_SQL);\n                statement.execute(CREATE_INDEX_SQL);\n            }\n            commitIfManaging();\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * Creates an account for {@code username} with {@code password}.\n         *\n         * <p>The password is hashed here and only the digest reaches the\n         * database. The caller's {@code char[]} is left untouched; clear it with\n         * {@link PasswordHasher#clear}.</p>\n         *\n         * @return {@link RegistrationOutcome#CREATED}, or\n         *         {@link RegistrationOutcome#USERNAME_ALREADY_EXISTS} if the\n         *         unique index rejected the row\n         * @throws IllegalArgumentException if the username or password fails\n         *                                  validation; nothing is written in\n         *                                  that case\n         * @throws SQLException             for any other database failure\n         */\n        public RegistrationOutcome register(String username, char[] password) throws SQLException {\n            requireValidUsername(username);\n            PasswordHasher.requireUsablePassword(password);\n\n            String digest = hasher.hash(password);\n            Timestamp now = Timestamp.valueOf(LocalDateTime.now());\n            try (PreparedStatement insert = connection.prepareStatement(INSERT_SQL)) {\n                insert.setString(1, username);\n                insert.setString(2, digest);\n                insert.setTimestamp(3, now);\n                insert.executeUpdate();\n            } catch (SQLException rejected) {\n                if (isUniqueViolation(rejected)) {\n                    // The index is the authority on uniqueness, so confirm the\n                    // loser of the race is really the same username before\n                    // reporting a friendly conflict.\n                    if (exists(username)) {\n                        rollbackIfManaging();\n                        return RegistrationOutcome.USERNAME_ALREADY_EXISTS;\n                    }\n                }\n                rollbackIfManaging();\n                throw rejected;\n            }\n            commitIfManaging();\n            return RegistrationOutcome.CREATED;\n        }\n\n        /**\n         * Verifies credentials that {@link #register} stored earlier.\n         *\n         * <p>Both failure outcomes cost the same PBKDF2 run: the unknown-user\n         * path is spent against {@link #guardDigest}. Render\n         * {@code NO_SUCH_USER} and {@code BAD_PASSWORD} identically to keep that\n         * equalisation meaningful.</p>\n         *\n         * @throws IllegalArgumentException if the username fails validation\n         * @throws SQLException             if the lookup fails\n         */\n        public AuthenticationOutcome authenticate(String username, char[] password) throws SQLException {\n            requireValidUsername(username);\n\n            String storedDigest = null;\n            boolean found = false;\n            try (PreparedStatement select = connection.prepareStatement(SELECT_DIGEST_SQL)) {\n                select.setString(1, username);\n                try (ResultSet rows = select.executeQuery()) {\n                    if (rows.next()) {\n                        found = true;\n                        storedDigest = rows.getString(1);\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n\n            if (password == null || password.length == 0) {\n                // Never let a blank submission skip the KDF: that would be a\n                // cheap way to tell \"user exists\" from \"user absent\" by timing.\n                hasher.matches(GUARD_PASSWORD, guardDigest);\n                return AuthenticationOutcome.BAD_PASSWORD;\n            }\n\n            if (!found) {\n                hasher.matches(GUARD_PASSWORD, guardDigest);\n                return AuthenticationOutcome.NO_SUCH_USER;\n            }\n            return hasher.matches(password, storedDigest)\n                    ? AuthenticationOutcome.AUTHENTICATED\n                    : AuthenticationOutcome.BAD_PASSWORD;\n        }\n\n        /** {@code true} if a row owns {@code username}. */\n        public boolean exists(String username) throws SQLException {\n            requireValidUsername(username);\n            try (PreparedStatement select = connection.prepareStatement(EXISTS_SQL)) {\n                select.setString(1, username);\n                try (ResultSet rows = select.executeQuery()) {\n                    return rows.next();\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        private void requireValidUsername(String username) {\n            if (username == null) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"username must not be null\");\n            }\n            if (!USERNAME_PATTERN.matcher(username).matches()) {\n                throw new IllegalArgumentException(\n                        \"username must be 3-64 characters of [A-Za-z0-9._-], got: \"\n                                + describe(username));\n            }\n        }\n\n        /** Length plus character-class summary, deliberately not the value itself. */\n        private static String describe(String username) {\n            return \"length \" + username.length();\n        }\n\n        /** SQL states for constraint violations: 23xxx in H2, PostgreSQL, MariaDB. */\n        private static boolean isUniqueViolation(SQLException failure) {\n            for (SQLException current = failure; current != null; current = current.getNextException()) {\n                String state = current.getSQLState();\n                if (state != null && state.startsWith(\"23\")) {\n                    return true;\n                }\n            }\n            return false;\n        }\n\n        private void commitIfManaging() throws SQLException {\n            if (commitOwnWork && !connection.getAutoCommit()) {\n                connection.commit();\n            }\n        }\n\n        private void rollbackIfManaging() {\n            if (!commitOwnWork) {\n                return;\n            }\n            try {\n                if (!connection.getAutoCommit()) {\n                    connection.rollback();\n                }\n            } catch (SQLException couldNotRollBack) {\n                // The original failure is the one worth reporting; a failed\n                // rollback on a dying connection must not mask it.\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Runnable demonstration against an in-memory (or caller-supplied)\n     * database.                                               boolean commitOwnWork) throws Exception {\n        String url = \"jdbc:h2:mem:tx\" + (commitOwnWork ? \"commit\" : \"manual\") + \";DB_CLOSE_DELAY=-1\";\n        try (Connection owner = DriverManager.getConnection(url, \"sa\", \"\")) {\n            owner.setAutoCommit(false);\n            UserRegistration.UserRegistry registry =\n                    new UserRegistration.UserRegistry(owner, hasher, commitOwnWork);\n            registry.ensureSchema();\n\n            char[] password = \"tx secret\".toCharArray();\n            check(\"register works UserRegistration.PasswordHasher.clear(password);\n\n            boolean visibleElsewhere;\n            try (Connection observer = DriverManager.getConnection(url, \"sa\", \"\")) {\n                observer.setAutoCommit(true);\n                visibleElsewhere = countRows(observer) > 0;\n            }\n            check(\"visibility from a second connection matches commitOwnWork=\" + commitOwnWork,\n                    visibleElsewhere == commitOwnWork);\n\n            if (!commitOwnWork) {\n                owner.rollback();\n                try (Connection observer = }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * The unknown-user path must cost a full key derivation, otherwise response\n     * time tells an attacker which usernames exist. Measured as a ratio over\n     * repeated runs so scheduler noise cannot decide the outcome.\n     */\n    private static void testUnknownUserTimingIsNotCheap(String base, UserRegistration.PasswordHasher unused)\n            throws Exception {\n        UserRegistration.PasswordHasher slow = new UserRegistration.PasswordHasher(TIMING_ITERATIONS);\n        try (Connection connection = DriverManager.getConnection(\"jdbc:h2:mem:timing;DB_CLOSE_DELAY=-1\", \"sa\", \"\")) {\n            UserRegistration.UserRegistry registry = new UserRegistration.UserRegistry(connection, slow);\n            registry.ensureSchema();\n            char[] enrolled = \"a genuinely long secret\".toCharArray();\n            registry.register(\"timing-user\", enrolled);\n            UserRegistration.PasswordHasher.clear(enrolled);\n\n            char[] wrongPassword = \"a genuinely long secrey\".toCharArray();\n            char[] absentPassword = \"a genuinely long secrez\".toCharArray();\n\n            // Warm both paths (JIT, H2 page cache) before measuring.\n            for (int i = 0; i < 2; i++) {\n                registry.authenticate(\"timing-user\", wrongPassword);\n                registry.authenticate(\"absent-user\", absentPassword);\n            }\n\n            long wrongBest = Long.MAX_VALUE;\n            long absentBest = Long.MAX_VALUE;\n            for (int i = 0; i < 5; i++) {\n                long start = System.nanoTime();\n                registry.authenticate(\"timing-user\", wrongPassword);\n                wrongBest = Math.min(wrongBest, System.nanoTime() - start);\n\n                start = System.nanoTime();\n                registry.authenticate(\"absent-user\", absentPassword);\n                absentBest = Math.min(absentBest, System.nanoTime() - start);\n            }\n            UserRegistration.PasswordHasher.clear(wrongPassword);\n            UserRegistration.PasswordHasher.clear(absentPassword);\n\n            double ratio = (double) absentBest / (double) wrongBest;\n            System.out.printf(\"      timing: wrong-password=%.0f ms, unknown-user=%.0f ms, ratio=%.2f%n\",\n                    wrongBest / 1e6, absentBest / 1e6, ratio);\n            check(\"a wrong password costs a full key derivation\",\n                    wrongBest / 1_000_000 >= TIMING_ITERATIONS / 20_000);\n            check(\"an unknown username is not answered cheaply (ratio >= 0.5)\", ratio >= 0.5);\n        }\n    }\n\n    //                            UserRegistration.PasswordHasher hasher)\n            throws SQLException {\n        UserRegistration.UserRegistry registry = new UserRegistration.UserRegistry(connection, hasher);\n        registry.ensureSchema();\n        try (Statement clear = connection.createStatement()) {\n            clear.executeUpdate(\"DELETE FROM app_users\");\n        }\n        return registry;\n    }\n\n    private static String digestOf(Connection connection, String username) throws SQLException {\n        return textOf(connection, \"SELECT password_hash FROM app_users WHERE connection.prepareStatement(sql)) {\n            select.setString(1, username);\n            try (ResultSet rows = select.executeQuery()) {\n                if (!rows.next()) {\n                    throw new IllegalStateException(\"no row for \" + username);\n                }\n                return rows.getString(1);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static int username = ?\")) {\n            count.setString(1, username);\n            try (ResultSet rows = count.executeQuery()) {\n                return rows.next() ? rows.getInt(1) : 0;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static int countRows(Connection connection) throws SQLException {\n        try (Statement statement = connection.createStatement();\n             ResultSet rows = It works on a JDBC\n`Connection` the caller already opened and owns.\n\n* Java 17, no dependencies beyond the JDK for the library itself.\n* One source file, no build tool required.\n\n---\n\n## Password handling\n\nThe password is never stored, never returned by any method, and never\nconcatenated into SQL. What reaches the database is a salted, stretched digest:\n\n```\npbkdf2-sha256$600000$Base64(16-byte salt)$Base64(32-byte digest)\n```\n\n| Decision | Why |\n| --- | --- |\n| **PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256, 600,000 iterations** | Stretches each guess so an offline crack of a stolen table is expensive. 600,000 is OWASP's 2023 floor for this primitive; measured at **860 ms** per derivation on one core of this machine. |\n| **Fresh 16-byte salt per account** (`SecureRandom`) | Two users with the same password get different rows, so one precomputed rainbow table cannot cover the table and identical rows do not leak who shares a secret. |\n| **Algorithm and iteration count stored in the row** | Verification uses the cost that was in force when the account was created, so raising the default later logs nobody out and invalidates nothing. |\n| **Stored-cost bounds (1,000 - 10,000,000)** | A tampered or corrupt row cannot ask for 1 round (instant cracking) or 10^9 rounds (login denial of service). |\n| **`MessageDigest.isEqual` for the digest compare** | Byte comparison that does not return early on the first differing byte. |\n| **Junk input fails closed** | A malformed, foreign-format, or unsupported stored digest yields \"does not match\", never an exception that reaches a login page and never an accidental match. |\n| **`char[]` in, `clear()` available** | Callers can wipe the secret instead of parking it in an immutable `String`. The library never modifies the caller's array and zeroes its own scratch (`PBEKeySpec`, derived key bytes) as soon as it is done. |\n| **Unknown usernames still pay for a derivation** | The lookup-miss path runs PBKDF2 against a throwaway digest, so response time cannot be used to enumerate which usernames have accounts. Measured ratio of unknown-user to wrong-password latency: **1.00**. |\n\n`String` is the wrong type for a password in Java: it is interned, may be\ncached, and cannot be overwritten. `char[]` is used for that reason. Zeroing it\nis still only best effort -- it narrows the window in which the secret is\nreachable in heap, it cannot retract a copy the JVM or your own code already\nmade, and it does nothing about swap.\n\n## SQL handling\n\n* Every statement that carries user-supplied data is a `PreparedStatement` with\n  `?` placeholders. A username such as `admin' OR '1'='1` is data, never\n  statement text. The only thing concatenated into SQL is the table name, a\n  compile-time constant.\n* The lookup compares with `username = ?`. Not `LIKE` -- under `LIKE`, `_` is a\n  single-character wildcard, so `alicex1` would satisfy a lookup for `alice_1`\n  and hand out an account name to guess against.\n* Usernames are matched case-sensitively and verbatim. No `UPPER()` folding\n  (`alice` must not be able to log in as `Alice`) and no trimming.\n* Usernames are validated against the allowlist `[A-Za-z0-9._-]{3,64}` before\n  any statement is prepared. Passwords must be non-empty and no longer than\n  1,024 characters, because PBKDF2 cost is linear in password length and an\n  unbounded field lets anyone make registration arbitrarily expensive.\n* Uniqueness is enforced by a `UNIQUE` index, not by a check-then-insert race.\n  The database is the authority; the loser of a concurrent signup gets\n  `USERNAME_ALREADY_EXISTS` and the existing row is left untouched.\n* `Statement`, `ResultSet` and `PreparedStatement` are closed with\n  try-with-resources. The caller's `Connection` is never closed.\n\n## Schema\n\n```sql\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS app_users (\n    id            BIGINT GENERATED BY DEFAULT AS IDENTITY PRIMARY KEY,\n    username      VARCHAR(64)  NOT NULL,\n    password_hash VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL,\n    created_at    TIMESTAMP    NOT NULL\n);\nCREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ux_app_users_username ON app_users (username);\n```\n\n`UserRegistry.ensureSchema()` runs exactly this DDL, so the table does not have\nto pre-exist. A digest is ~90 characters, which leaves room in `VARCHAR(200)`\nfor longer iteration counts. The DDL is portable across H2, PostgreSQL, MariaDB\nand MySQL 8; on Oracle/SQL Server adjust the identity and timestamp clauses.\n\n## API\n\n```java\npublic final class UserRegistration {\n    public enum RegistrationOutcome   { CREATED, USERNAME_ALREADY_EXISTS }\n    public enum AuthenticationOutcome { AUTHENTICATED, NO_SUCH_USER, BAD_PASSWORD }\n\n    public static final class PasswordHasher {          // thread-safe\n        public PasswordHasher();                        // 600,000 iterations\n        public PasswordHasher(int iterations);\n        public String  hash(char[] password);           // the value to store\n        public boolean matches(char[] password, String storedDigest);\n        public static void clear(char[] password);      // best-effort wipe\n    }\n\n    public static final class UserRegistry {            // one per Connection\n        public UserRegistry(Connection connection, PasswordHasher hasher);\n        public UserRegistry(Connection connection, PasswordHasher hasher, boolean commitOwnWork);\n        public void ensureSchema() throws SQLException;\n        public RegistrationOutcome   register(String username, char[] password) throws SQLException;\n        public AuthenticationOutcome authenticate(String username, char[] password) throws SQLException;\n        public boolean exists(String username) throws SQLException;\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Typical use\n\n```java\n// One hasher for the whole application; it is thread-safe.\nUserRegistration.PasswordHasher hasher = new UserRegistration.PasswordHasher();\n\n// One registry per connection, i.e. typically one per request.\nUserRegistration.UserRegistry registry = new UserRegistration.UserRegistry(connection, hasher);\n\nchar[] password = readPasswordFromConsole();       // e.g. System.console().readPassword()\ntry {\n    UserRegistration.RegistrationOutcome outcome = registry.register(\"alice\", password);\n    if (outcome == UserRegistration.RegistrationOutcome.USERNAME_ALREADY_EXISTS) {\n        // ask for a different username\n    }\n} finally {\n    UserRegistration.PasswordHasher.clear(password);\n}\n```\n\n`IllegalArgumentException` means the input was rejected and nothing was written;\n`SQLException` means the database failed.\n\n### Login\n\n`AuthenticationOutcome.NO_SUCH_USER` and `BAD_PASSWORD` are kept distinct for\nserver-side logging and migration tooling. **Show the caller one identical\nmessage for both** (\"invalid username or password\"), log the distinction\nserver-side only, and put rate limiting and lockout in front of this call.\nDistinguishable wording or response sizes turn the timing equalisation above\nback into a username oracle.\n\n### Transactions\n\n`register` does not open or close a transaction on its own. If the connection\nhas manual commits and the registry was built with `commitOwnWork = true` (the\ndefault), a successful insert is committed and a failed one rolled back. Pass\n`false` when the surrounding unit of work decides when to commit; the rollback\nthen belongs to that unit of work too.\n\n---\n\n## Compile and run\n\nEverything below was run on OpenJDK 17.0.20.1 on this machine. Sources are\nASCII-only, so no `-encoding` flag is needed (the platform default charset here\nis US-ASCII).\n\n### Run the demonstration\n\nThe demo needs a JDBC driver; H2 is used here only so the example is runnable.\n\n```bash\njavac -d out UserRegistration.java\njava -cp out:h2.jar UserRegistration\n```\n\nOptionally point it at your own database:\n`java -cp out:h2.jar UserRegistration <jdbc-url> <user> <password>`.\n\nActual output on this machine:\n\n```\nregister alice            -> CREATED\nregister alice (twice)    -> USERNAME_ALREADY_EXISTS\nregister bob              -> CREATED\nauthenticate alice/right  -> AUTHENTICATED\nauthenticate alice/wrong  -> BAD_PASSWORD\nauthenticate carol/right  -> NO_SUCH_USER\nregister quoted payload -> refused by validation: username must be 3-64 characters of [A-Za-z0-9._-], got: length 16\nregister admin--          -> CREATED\nauthenticate admin--/right-> AUTHENTICATED\nauthenticate admin (never registered, suffix must not act as a comment) -> NO_SUCH_USER\n\nstored rows (note: no password appears below):\n  alice  ->  pbkdf2-sha256$1000$Hkc5irU/3b6i9XlnKiX3sQ==$jY/vdLsd6+E/lsi8rQrGTsHbkd85k2rZTaUXpvSIZKo=\n  bob  ->  pbkdf2-sha256$1000$LGUJBCOGS1BVyWrMheZaSw==$HpgMauunv2xuYt8/UtpfwFyjM93LqKo/ZBO8ZE1GzeQ=\n  admin--  ->  pbkdf2-sha256$1000$VBMEk6nbigXew5Um9pOEBw==$yjEkzDj/UXFx5t/DPITLgSHkGjKYO1Yjo/zO4CpU3zw=\n```\n\nThe demo builds its hasher with 1,000 iterations so it finishes immediately; a\nproduction `new PasswordHasher()` gets the 600,000-iteration default. Note that\n`alice` and `bob` received different salts, and that `admin--` is stored as a\nliteral username -- the trailing `--` does not act as a SQL comment because it\nnever enters the statement text.\n\n### Run the verification suites\n\nTwo suites, deliberately complementary:\n\n```bash\njavac -Xlint:all -Werror -d out \\\n    UserRegistration.java UserRegistrationH2Test.java UserRegistrationSqlContractTest.java\n\n# 1. SQL contract suite: no database, JDBC doubles via java.lang.reflect.Proxy.\njava -cp out UserRegistrationSqlContractTest\n\n# 2. Integration suite against a real engine.\njava -cp out:h2.jar UserRegistrationH2Test\n```\n\nCurrent results: **85 checks passed** and **84 checks passed** respectively\n(exit code 0; on failure each prints every failed check and exits 1).\n\n* `UserRegistrationSqlContractTest` records the exact statement text and every\n  bound parameter, so it can assert things no database would tell you: that a\n  credential appears nowhere in the statement text, that all three placeholders\n  were bound, that the data path never uses a plain `Statement`, that a\n  duplicate-key `SQLException` (state `23505`) becomes a conflict rather than a\n  retry, that a `22001` \"value too long\" is *not* mistaken for a conflict, that\n  every statement is closed, and that the unknown-username path really does pay\n  for a derivation (~178 ms against a doubled connection, where everything else\n  is free).\n* `UserRegistrationH2Test` runs the same calls against H2, which is what catches\n  a predicate that now matches *too much*: the `LIKE` and `UPPER()` variants\n  above, transaction visibility from a second connection, and end-to-end\n  register/authenticate/duplicate behaviour on real rows. It also measures the\n  enumeration-timing equalisation as a ratio of best-of-5 runs, so scheduler\n  noise cannot decide the outcome.\n\nBoth suites were mutation-tested: 16 weakenings of the implementation (store the\npassword in the clear, `=` to `LIKE`, case-folded lookup, drop the timing guard,\ndrop the iteration-count bounds, drop username validation, fail open on an\nunknown user, treat any constraint violation as a duplicate, forget the commit,\nshared salt, `==` instead of `MessageDigest.isEqual`, and more) were each applied\nindividually and confirmed to turn at least one check red. Results in *Verified\nbehaviour* below.\n\n---\n\n## What this deliberately does not include\n\nThe task scoped a connection and credential storage. These are still required\nbefore this is a login system, and none of them belong in this file:\n\n* **Transport security.** Digest strength is irrelevant if the password crosses\n  the network in cleartext: TLS is the application's job.\n* **Rate limiting, lockout, and breach detection.** Nothing here throttles\n  repeated guesses against one account.\n* **Password policy and strength checking.** Only emptiness and length are\n  enforced; a minimum length, a common-password blocklist and a breach check\n  (`haveibeenpwned` k-anonymity API) are a product decision.\n* **A migration tool.** `ensureSchema()` is a convenience; run the DDL through\n  Flyway/Liquibase in anything real.\n* **Alternative KDFs.** Argon2id and scrypt are stronger choices when a library\n  is available, since they also resist GPU and ASIC cracking. This file sticks to\n  PBKDF2 because it is in every JDK and needs no third-party dependency; the\n  stored format already carries an algorithm tag, so a second algorithm can be\n  added to `PasswordHasher` without touching existing rows.\n* **Session issuance.** `authenticate` answers a question; it does not create a\n  session, cookie or token.\n* **Charset normalisation of usernames.** The allowlist keeps this moot\n  (ASCII-only). It pins three things at once: the KDF\n     * parameters are honoured, and this JVM's {@code PBEKeySpec} folds\n     * {@code char[]} to bytes as UTF-8 rather than UTF-16BE -- a fact that\n     * decides whether a digest survives a JVM change or a migration to another\n     * stack, and that cannot be observed any other way from inside the code.\n     */\n    private static void testKnownAnswerVector() {\n        UserRegistration.PasswordHasher hasher = new UserRegistration.PasswordHasher(1_000);\n        char[] password = new char[]{'c', 'a', 'f', (char) 0xE9};\n        String expected = \"pbkdf2-sha256$1000$MDEyMzQ1Njc4OWFiY2RlZg==\"\n                + \"$SpPbMsB2Jp2sIgwjl5JBGZLxjqpRBAPmJ99m8fIg+dg=\";\n\n        byte[] fixedSalt = decodeBase64(\"MDEyMzQ1Njc4OWFiY2RlZg==\");\n        check(\"the fixed salt is 16 bytes of ASCII\",\n                fixedSalt.length == 16 && new String(fixedSalt, java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.US_ASCII)\n                        .equals(\"0123456789abcdef\"));\n\n        check(\"the digest matches an independent implementation\",\n                expected.equals(hashWithSalt(hasher, password, fixedSalt)));\n        check(\"the known-answer digest verifies\", hasher.matches(password, expected));\n\n        char[] lookalikeUtf16 = new char[]{'c', 'a', 'f', (char) 0xE9, (char) 0x00};\n        check(\"a password with a trailing NUL is a different secret\",\n                !hasher.matches(lookalikeUtf16, expected));\n        UserRegistration.PasswordHasher.clear(password);\n        UserRegistration.PasswordHasher.clear(lookalikeUtf16);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Re-derives {@code hash()} with a caller-supplied salt, so a fixed salt can\n     * be used against a published vector. javax.crypto.spec.PBEKeySpec spec =\n                    new javax.crypto.spec.PBEKeySpec(password, salt, 1_000, 256);\n            byte[] digest = javax.crypto.SecretKeyFactory\n                    .getInstance(UserRegistration.PasswordHasher.JCA_ALGORITHM)\n                    .generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();\n            spec.clearPassword();\n            return UserRegistration.PasswordHasher.FORMAT_ID + \"$1000$\"\n                    + java.util.Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(salt) + \"$\"\n                    + java.util.Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(digest);\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException failure) {\n            throw new IllegalStateException(failure);\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static byte[] decodeBase64(String value) {\n        try {\n            return java.util.Base64.getDecoder().decode(value);\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | IllegalArgumentException impossible) {\n            throw new IllegalStateException(impossible);\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static void testHashIsNotIdempotent(UserRegistration.PasswordHasher hasher) {\n        char[] password = \"one secret\".toCharArray();\n        String second = hasher.hash(password);\n        check(\"hashing the same password twice gives different values\", !first.equals(second));\n of 18 caught; two things to address:\n\n1. **M12_lax_base64 SURVIVED** — switching `Base64.getDecoder()` to `Base64.getMimeDecoder()` survived all checks. Why? My malformed test includes `\"not-base64!\"` → MIME decoder ignores the `!` and decodes \"notbase64\"?? MIME decoder discards chars outside the alphabet. \"notbase64\" is 9 valid chars → decodes to 6 bytes → salt length != 16 → rejected. So the length check saved it. And \"AAAA\" 3-byte salt → still 3 bytes → rejected. So MIME vs strict doesn't change outcomes for my inputs because the *length* assertions dominate. To make it assertable, I need an input where strict rejects and MIME accepts producing the *right lengths*: e.g. salt field `\"AB\\r\\nCD...\"` with whitespace/newlines inserted — strict rejects (line breaks not allowed in basic decoder... actually basic decoder rejects \\r\\n? Basic decoder rejects any char not in alphabet including whitespace), MIME strips them and decodes fine. So: a digest whose salt field contains an embedded newline would be *accepted* by MIME and *rejected* by strict. Does that matter security-wise? It means a corrupt/tampered row could smuggle a canonicalized variant; more concretely it shows the decoder's strictness is load-bearing for \"malformed fails closed\". It's a real (if modest) robustness property: accepting foreign characters in a stored credential field means two different stored strings can denote the same credential, and a `==` on the string elsewhere (e.g. migration dedup, or a \"password changed?\" check) would disagree with the crypto. Worth an assertion.\n\n   Add to malformed list: a digest with an embedded CR/LF inside the salt field, and one with a URL-safe alphabet (`-`/`_`) which the basic decoder rejects but MIME also rejects (both reject), so not useful. Also `+`/`/` vs `-_`. Let me use: `\"pbkdf2-sha256$1000$MDAy MzQ1Njc4OWFiY2RlZg==$...\"` with a space — wait, space is not in MIME alphabet either? MIME decoder discards all non-alphabet chars including spaces and \\r\\n. So `\"MDEy\\rMzQ1Njc4OWFiY2RlZg==\"` → MIME decodes to 16 bytes fine; strict rejects. \n\n   So add that vector; the check \"malformed digest cannot authenticate\" will pass for strict (rejects → false) and *fail* for MIME (accepts, and if the rest matches the password, it authenticates!). Measured on\nOpenJDK 17.0.20.1 by deriving with the raw JCE (same salt/iterations, digests\nidentical within each group):\n\n* `\"café\"`, `\"café\"+NUL`, `\"café\"+NUL+NUL` -> all `4a93db32…f220f9d8`\n* `\"q\"+U+D800`, `\"q\"+U+D801`, `\"q\"+U+DC00`, `\"q\"+\"?\"` -> all `460aaca2…4b1a7b5a`\n\nEach group is a set of *different* passwords that produce the *same* digest, so a\nuser who registered one can be logged in by anyone presenting another. This is\ninvisible to a password-hashing wrapper and not mentioned by `PBEKeySpec`'s docs.\n\n**Not affected:** interior NULs (only *trailing* ones are dropped — `\"pa\\0ss\"` is\ndistinct), and correctly paired surrogate pairs (astral/emoji are fine).\n`\"café\"+space` and `\"caf\"+NUL+\"é\"` are distinct, so this is specifically\ntrailing-NUL and unpaired-surrogate.\n\n**How to apply:** before hashing, reject a password ending in `NUL` or containing\nan unpaired surrogate (`Character.isHighSurrogate(c)` not followed by\n`Character.isLowSurrogate`). Reject, don't sanitize — trimming silently changes\nthe secret. Then pin it with a test that derives through the *raw* JCE to prove\nthe collision is real, so the guard can't be deleted or half-deleted silently.\n\n**Second-order trap this creates:** once such a candidate is refused *before* the\nKDF, a lookup-miss path that pays a dummy derivation for timing equalisation no\nlonger covers it — the refusal returns instantly, re-opening the username-\nenumeration oracle (`q\"+U+D800` + fast answer == that user exists). Charge any\nunverifiable candidate the dummy derivation too, and measure that ratio.\n\nRelated: `jdk-lenient-key-and-base64-apis` (the same \"JCE is lenient, validate\nyourself\" shape), `time-bounded-refusal-assertions`.\n","file_path":"/root/.qwen/memories/reference/pbekeyspec-char-array-not-injective.md"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","usage":{"input_tokens":145720,"output_tokens":691,"cache_read_input_tokens":0,"total_tokens":146411}}},{"type":"user","uuid":"e887244a-298d-4dcb-9aa3-7bbe63e8cf79","session_id":"30d7d6ea-ee4c-42ab-b1e6-05c32a6442ce","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_640ab521caae403f8b7b0cbe","is_error":false,"content":"Successfully created and wrote to new file: /root/.qwen/memories/reference/pbekeyspec-char-array-not-injective.md."}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"aba7ed7e-dd19-4f36-b878-2116464b1dd6","session_id":"30d7d6ea-ee4c-42ab-b1e6-05c32a6442ce","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"aba7ed7e-dd19-4f36-b878-2116464b1dd6","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I need to check the current state before continuing. And run mutation batteries in the background with incremental output, never as a foreground\ncall with a timeout.\n\n**Why:** Measured 2026-09-12. Measured on this JVM: trailing NULs are discarded and every unpaired surrogate maps to a single `?`, so `café`, `café+NUL`, and `café+NUL+NUL` derive the *same* digest — as do U+D800, U+D801, U+DC00 and a literal `?`.
